| where DstHostname has_any ("budibase") // scope to Budibase hosts; then exclude known monitoring source IPs and staging hosts via a watchlist join
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the Budibase app log / DB user record for the account the session resolved to — check whether it is a pre-existing local (password) admin now showing providerType=oidc and whether the IdP sub differs from any prior link. 2) Contain: revoke the budibase:auth session, force-logout the account, and disable the affected OIDC provider config until email_verified enforcement is confirmed; reset the victim admin. 3) Hunt: enumerate all accounts whose providerType flipped to oidc without a matching prior sub, and review the trusted IdP (Keycloak/Authentik) for self-registrations or profile email edges asserting internal addresses with email_verified=false.

Stacked SQL-injection payload against Budibase datasource query API (multipleStatements) AIExploitation Medium
Hunts inbound HTTP requests to Budibase's datasource/query REST endpoints carrying stacked SQL (a semicolon-separated second statement) with destructive/exfil keywords such as DROP TABLE, DELETE, GRANT ALL or INTO OUTFILE. This is article-specific because the vuln (GHSA-q6x4-v3qx-85qw) is that @budibase/server <=3.38.1 sets multipleStatements:true, so a single injected `;` executes a second attacker-chosen statement. Rationale: Uses the two invariant tokens of this vuln: the Budibase query API path (/api/queries, /api/datasources) and the stacked-statement pattern (`;` + DROP TABLE / INTO OUTFILE / GRANT ALL) that multipleStatements:true uniquely allows. Cross-checked against the GitLab advisory mirror and OffSeq radar entry. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/@budibase/server/GHSA-q6x4-v3qx-85qw/ • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/server-budibase-sql-injection-via-multiplestatements-true-3b00631cb39a1028 • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/budibase/GHSA-qqf5-x7mj-v43p/
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Security scanners / DAST tools (Burp, sqlmap, Nessus) probing the Budibase instance in an authorized pentest
  • Legitimate Budibase query builder where an admin genuinely writes a query containing the word DELETE or DROP as part of app logic
  • WAF learning/tuning mode replaying captured payloads
  • A user field legitimately containing text like 'grant all access' being URL-encoded into the request
  • Automated regression tests that exercise datasource CRUD endpoints
Suppression filter
| where not(SourceIP in ("10.0.0.0/8")) // exclude sanctioned internal scanner ranges; replace with your DAST subnet
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full request from the WAF/proxy log and confirm the second statement after `;` is attacker-controlled (DROP/DELETE/OUTFILE/GRANT) rather than app-legitimate text; note source IP, auth state and whether it hit /api/queries/preview. 2) Contain: block the source IP at the WAF, disable the affected MySQL datasource in Budibase, and upgrade @budibase/server past 3.38.1 (or hot-patch multipleStatements:false in packages/server/src/integrations/mysql.ts). 3) Hunt: review MySQL general/audit logs for stacked statements from the Budibase DB user, and run UC-2 to check whether mysqld wrote any INTO OUTFILE dumps to /tmp or the web root.

MySQL server (mysqld) writes exfil file via SELECT INTO OUTFILE on Budibase DB host AIActions on Objectives Medium
Hunts the mysqld process creating files outside its data directory — the server-side footprint of a successful SELECT INTO OUTFILE data-theft chain that the Budibase multipleStatements SQL injection enables. Article-specific because the advisory explicitly lists 'Exfiltrate data via SELECT INTO OUTFILE' as an impact, and mysqld writing to /tmp, a web root, or Temp is anomalous for a normal DB workload. Rationale: Pivots on the advisory's named exfil primitive (SELECT INTO OUTFILE) by detecting mysqld itself creating files outside /var/lib/mysql - a behaviour that normal Budibase-driven MySQL workloads never exhibit. Excludes the datadir to keep the signal clean. Cross-checked against: • https://advisories.gitlab.com/npm/@budibase/server/GHSA-q6x4-v3qx-85qw/ • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/server-budibase-sql-injection-via-multiplestatements-true-3b00631cb39a1028
ATT&CKT1190 T1005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • DBA using SELECT INTO OUTFILE for a legitimate scheduled data export to a reporting directory
  • mysqldump or backup tooling writing dumps to /tmp or a staging path
  • MySQL error/slow-query logs configured to write outside the datadir
  • Application code that legitimately exports CSVs via the DB engine
  • Migration jobs writing .sql files during a maintenance window
Suppression filter
| where not(FolderPath has_any ("/var/backups/", "/opt/mysql-exports/")) // exclude sanctioned DBA export/backup directories
Response runbook
1) Triage: identify the created file, open it read-only to confirm it contains dumped table rows, and correlate its creation time against UC-1 WAF hits / the Budibase datasource user's MySQL query log. 2) Contain: preserve then remove the exfil file, disable the Budibase MySQL datasource, set secure_file_priv to a locked-down path (or NULL to disable OUTFILE), and revoke FILE privilege from the Budibase DB account. 3) Hunt: search web-server and proxy egress for retrieval of the written file, review MySQL logs for other stacked statements from the same session, and confirm no DROP/DELETE (data destruction) statements executed.

OpenAM /authservice CustomCallback ClassName XML — CVE-2026-62379 pre-auth Class.forName RCE AIExploitation High
Hunts inbound PLL remote-auth requests to OpenAM's /authservice endpoint whose XML body carries a <CustomCallback>/<ClassName> element — the exact vector CVE-2026-62379 abuses to make the server Class.forName-load and instantiate an attacker-named Java class without authentication. Article-specific because it keys on the two OpenAM remote-auth DTD tags that drive the vulnerable code path, not generic 'suspicious POST'. Rationale: Keys on the concrete OpenAM PLL remote-auth tokens `/authservice` + `<CustomCallback>`/`<ClassName>` that AuthXMLUtils.createCustomCallback passes to Class.forName (per the GHSA and OffSeq/GitLab write-ups); a POST to that endpoint carrying those XML tags is the exploit itself, not a generic probe. Body match requires a proxy/WAF that logs request payloads. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wg5r-wc3x-39vc • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/orgopenidentityplatformopenamopenam-core-openam-unauthenticated-remote-code-execution-via-classforname-7678e3fe5efb8e5c • https://advisories.gitlab.com/maven/org.openidentityplatform.openam/openam-scripting/CVE-2026-47424/
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimately configured distributed-auth UI or policy agents that POST CustomCallback XML to /authservice when a site genuinely uses custom callback modules
  • Vulnerability scanners (Nessus/Qualys/Nuclei) probing the endpoint post-disclosure
  • Internal red-team / patch-validation testing of CVE-2026-62379
  • Load-balancer or uptime health checks hitting /authservice (these lack the CustomCallback/ClassName body, so the body filter suppresses them)
Suppression filter
| where not (RequestClientApplication has_any ("Nessus","Qualys","Nuclei","nikto") )
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full request body for the matched event; inspect the <ClassName> value — a JDK/attacker-controlled class (e.g. anything not an OpenAM callback package) plus an external/unauthenticated SourceIP is a true positive. Confirm the target OpenAM build is <=16.1.1. 2) Contain: block the SourceIP at the WAF, enable sunRemoteAuthSecurityEnabled to force the remote-auth security token, and restrict external access to /authservice; schedule the 16.1.2 upgrade. 3) Hunt: pivot to the OpenAM host and check for java/tomcat spawning shells (UC2), new .jsp files under webapps, and outbound connections from the app-server user in the minutes following the request.

OpenAM/Tomcat java process spawning shell or LOLBin — CVE-2026-62379 post-exploitation AIInstallation High
Detects the OpenAM app-server JVM (java/javaw, Tomcat/Catalina) spawning an interactive shell or download/recon LOLBin — the immediate on-host consequence of CVE-2026-62379 instantiating attacker code via Class.forName. Scoped to OpenAM by requiring openam/catalina/tomcat/forgerock in the parent command line so it isn't a generic 'java spawns cmd' rule. Rationale: OpenAM is a Java/Tomcat app, so successful CVE-2026-62379 code execution surfaces as the JVM parenting a shell/LOLBin; anchoring the parent command line to openam/catalina/tomcat/forgerock keeps it OpenAM-specific and strips generic java build-tool noise. Cross-checked against the GHSA advisory and OffSeq threat radar entry describing full-server code execution. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-wg5r-wc3x-39vc • https://radar.offseq.com/threat/orgopenidentityplatformopenamopenam-core-openam-unauthenticated-remote-code-execution-via-classforname-7678e3fe5efb8e5c
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.003 T1059.004
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • OpenAM/Tomcat lifecycle scripts (catalina.sh start/stop) that legitimately fork a shell during startup or shutdown
  • ssoadm / amster CLI or backup cron jobs invoked through the app-server user that shell out
  • Monitoring/APM agents embedded in the JVM that exec helper binaries (e.g. curl for health probes)
  • Admin running diagnostic commands (whoami/net) under the tomcat service account during maintenance
Suppression filter
| where ProcessCommandLine !has_any ("catalina.sh start","catalina.sh stop","catalina.sh run","shutdown","ssoadm","amster")
Response runbook
1) Triage: examine the child ProcessCommandLine — recon (whoami/id/net), downloaders (curl/wget/certutil to external hosts), or reverse-shell one-liners under the tomcat/openam service account are true positives; benign catalina start/stop is not. Correlate the timestamp against UC1 /authservice hits on the same host. 2) Contain: isolate the host, kill the offending process tree, rotate OpenAM service-account and datastore/admin credentials, and enable sunRemoteAuthSecurityEnabled while scheduling the 16.1.2 upgrade. 3) Hunt: look for dropped .jsp/.class webshells under Tomcat webapps, new cron/systemd or scheduled-task persistence created by the service user, and outbound C2 from the JVM.

OpenAM WebAuthn pre-auth Java deserialization payload to /json/authenticate (CVE-2026-62263) AIExploitation High
Hunts inbound HTTP requests to OpenAM's WebAuthn authentication endpoint carrying a base64-encoded Java serialized object (magic bytes AC ED 00 05 => 'rO0AB'). The vuln's ObjectInputFilter allows the AuthenticatorImpl root but leaves the nested gadget graph unchecked, so the attacker delivers the gadget chain inside a serialized authenticator via an attacker-chosen userHandle — pre-auth. A serialized-object marker on the authenticate endpoint is near-unambiguous exploitation. Rationale: Uses the article's exact vuln surface: OpenAM WebAuthn authenticator deserialization reached pre-auth via userHandle on the /json/authenticate endpoint. The 'rO0AB' token is the base64 of the Java serialization magic (0xAC 0xED 0x00 0x05) named implicitly by the deserialization sink; its presence on the authenticate endpoint is exploitation, not normal WebAuthn traffic (which sends base64url userHandle, not serialized graphs). defender_kql/falcon/datadog/cloudwatch left empty: this is server-side web-payload telemetry that lives on a WAF/reverse proxy, not Defender AH, FDR, or AWS. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-gf8h-gq53-288j • https://advisories.gitlab.com/maven/org.openidentityplatform.openam/openam-auth-webauthn/CVE-2026-45051/ • https://vulnerability.circl.lu/vuln/ghsa-6c99-87fr-6q7r • https://github.com/OpenIdentityPlatform/OpenAM/wiki/How-to-Setup-WebAuthn-Authentication-in-OpenAM
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Authorized pentest / red-team probing OpenAM with ysoserial or Nuclei deserialization templates (true positive, confirm authorization)
  • Internal vulnerability scanner (Tenable/Qualys) sending crafted deserialization test payloads to the auth endpoint
  • Legitimate WebAuthn logins — these send base64url userHandle values and will NOT contain the 'rO0AB' serialized marker, so they do not match
  • WAF that base64-encodes and re-logs an entire request in a field that happens to already contain a serialized cookie from a different app
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Pull the full request from the WAF/proxy log (RequestContext/Message). Confirm the payload after 'rO0AB' is a serialized graph and identify the SourceIP, whether the request got HTTP 200/500, and if it was pre-auth (no valid session). 2) CONTAIN: Block the SourceIP at the WAF, take the exposed OpenAM node out of the LB pool, and confirm the org is on openam-auth-webauthn <=16.1.1 (vulnerable) — upgrade past 16.1.1. 3) HUNT: On the OpenAM host, run UC2 (java spawning shell children) for the same time window; review Tomcat/catalina logs for exceptions during readObject; check for new files, cron/systemd persistence, and outbound connections from the Java process.
// OpenAM WebAuthn deserialization RCE - base64 Java serialized object ('rO0AB' = 0xACED0005) to the authenticate endpoint
CommonSecurityLog
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where RequestURL has_any ("/openam/json/authenticate", "/json/authenticate", "/openam/json/realms", "/json/realms")
| where RequestContext has "rO0AB" or Message has "rO0AB" or RequestURL has "rO0AB" or RequestCookies has "rO0AB"
| project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceUserName, DestinationHostName, RequestMethod, RequestURL, RequestContext, Message, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct
| order by TimeGenerated desc

OpenAM/Tomcat Java process spawning a shell — post-deserialization RCE (CVE-2026-62263) AIExploitation High
Hunts the OpenAM JVM (java/java.exe under Tomcat/catalina) spawning a command interpreter or download tool — the payload-execution stage after the WebAuthn gadget chain fires during readObject(). An IAM/SSO appliance's Java process spawning cmd/powershell/sh/curl is abnormal and high-fidelity for RCE. Rationale: Grounded in the article's sink (WebAuthn readObject/readResolve executes during readObject() before any assertion check) and the affected package org.openidentityplatform.openam. The parent-context filter (openam/catalina/tomcat/org.openidentityplatform in the JVM cmdline) pins this to the OpenAM appliance rather than any JVM, and pairing it with a shell/downloader child is the classic Java-deser RCE process shape. Cross-platform (java on Linux, java.exe on Windows) since OpenAM commonly runs on Tomcat/Linux. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-gf8h-gq53-288j • https://advisories.gitlab.com/maven/org.openidentityplatform.openam/openam-auth-webauthn/CVE-2026-45051/ • https://vulnerability.circl.lu/vuln/ghsa-6c99-87fr-6q7r
ATT&CKT1059.004 T1059.001 T1190
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Tomcat startup/shutdown wrappers where catalina.sh/java invokes 'sh -c' during normal service lifecycle
  • OpenAM plugin or scripted auth module that legitimately calls Runtime.exec for a health-check or LDAP helper script
  • Sysadmin running diagnostics under the tomcat/openam service account (whoami, curl to an internal health URL)
  • Monitoring/backup agent shelling out from the JVM for metrics collection
Suppression filter
| where not (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("catalina.sh start","catalina.sh stop","catalina.sh run","-version","/usr/bin/id -u","/healthcheck","/monitoring/"))
Response runbook
1) TRIAGE: Inspect the child ProcessCommandLine — decode any base64/encoded command, and check whether it is a known startup script (catalina.sh) or a recon command (whoami, id, curl to attacker host). Correlate the timestamp against UC1 hits (serialized payload to /json/authenticate) on the same host. 2) CONTAIN: Isolate the OpenAM host, kill the offending child and its descendants, and rotate OpenAM service-account and any credentials the JVM could read (LDAP bind, datastore, secrets). 3) HUNT: Search for webshells under the Tomcat webapps dir, new persistence (cron, systemd, Run keys), outbound C2 from the java process, and lateral auth from the OpenAM host; confirm the fix (openam-auth-webauthn > 16.1.1) is deployed everywhere.

Node.js host process spawning OS shell (Nunjucks SSTI child_process.execSync RCE) AIExploitation Medium
Hunts a Node.js runtime process spawning cmd.exe/powershell or a Unix shell, the direct result of an attacker-controlled .prompty template abusing @prompty/core's Nunjucks renderer to reach global.process.mainModule.require('child_process').execSync(). This is article-specific because the RCE terminates in a node→shell parent/child chain that the vulnerable renderer produces. Rationale: Anchored on the corroborated Nunjucks RCE payload chain (range.constructor -> global.process.mainModule.require('child_process').execSync) whose default execSync behaviour spawns cmd.exe /d /s /c on Windows and /bin/sh -c on Linux, producing a node->shell parent/child pair. Cross-checked PayloadsAllTheThings and HackTricks SSTI-JS references plus the GHSA advisory. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6 • https://github.com/swisskyrepo/PayloadsAllTheThings/blob/master/Server%20Side%20Template%20Injection/JavaScript.md • https://hacktricks.wiki/en/pentesting-web/ssti-server-side-template-injection/index.html
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.003 T1059.004
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Legitimate Node build tooling (npm/yarn/webpack) invoking cmd.exe /c or /bin/sh -c for lifecycle scripts
  • Electron or CLI apps built on Node that shell out to system utilities by design
  • node-based process managers (pm2, nodemon) restarting apps via a shell
  • CI/CD runners executing shell steps under a Node orchestrator
  • Developer workstations running local server frameworks that exec git/openssl via child_process
Suppression filter
| where not (InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("npm-cli.js","yarn.js","webpack","nodemon","pm2","node-gyp") or ProcessCommandLine has_any ("npm run","prepare","postinstall","node_modules\\.bin"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full child command line and InitiatingProcessCommandLine — a benign build shows npm/yarn lifecycle strings; malicious shows ad-hoc recon (whoami, id, curl, /dev/tcp reverse shells) or base64. Check whether the node process was serving/rendering .prompty content. 2) Contain: isolate the host, kill the node PID, and block outbound to any IP the spawned shell contacted; snapshot for IR. 3) Hunt: confirm @prompty/core version on the host (UC2), review recently written/downloaded .prompty files for constructor/prototype/child_process tokens, and pivot on the same node process for further shell spawns or network egress.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","node")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","sh","bash","dash","zsh")
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("/d /s /c","/c "," -c ")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where ParentProcessName has "node"
| where TargetProcessName has_any ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","/sh","/bash","/dash","/zsh","bash","sh")
| where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("/d /s /c","/c "," -c ")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: 6f1e9c2a-7b3d-4e88-9a11-2c4d5e6f7a8b
description: Detects a Node.js runtime spawning a command interpreter, consistent with @prompty/core Nunjucks SSTI reaching child_process.execSync (GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6).
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6
  - https://github.com/swisskyrepo/PayloadsAllTheThings/blob/master/Server%20Side%20Template%20Injection/JavaScript.md
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/07/27

  - attack.t1059.004
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\node.exe'
      - '/node'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
      - '/sh'
      - '/bash'
      - '/dash'
      - '/zsh'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - '/d /s /c'
      - '/c '
      - ' -c '
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child and selection_cmd
level: medium
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @ParentImage:(*\node.exe OR *\Node.exe OR *\NODE.EXE) @Image:(*\cmd.exe OR *\CMD.EXE OR *\powershell.exe OR *\PowerShell.exe OR *\pwsh.exe OR *\Pwsh.exe)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^node(\.exe)?$/i
| ImageFileName=/(cmd|powershell|pwsh)\.exe$|\/(sh|bash|dash|zsh)$/i
| regex("(/d /s /c|/c | -c )", field=CommandLine)
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

Vulnerable @prompty/core package present in node_modules (GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6 exposure) AIDelivery Medium
Surfaces hosts and build images where the vulnerable @prompty/core npm package is installed by matching its node_modules folder path. Directly implements the advisory's remediation-triage guidance to locate the package before an untrusted .prompty template can trigger SSTI-to-RCE. Rationale: Uses the exact vulnerable package coordinate from the advisory (npm @prompty/core, <=0.1.4 and <=2.0.0-beta.4) matched via its canonical node_modules/@prompty/core install path, directly serving the advisory's 'check whether @prompty/core is present in manifests, lockfiles, or build images' remediation step. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-w28w-gp39-m4p6
ATT&CKT1190 T1195.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Hosts already upgraded to patched @prompty/core 2.0.0-beta.5+ (path alone cannot confirm version — verify package.json)
  • Legitimate developer workstations that intentionally depend on @prompty/core
  • CI/CD build agents that transiently install the package during pipeline runs
  • Container image layers cached on a node showing the folder without runtime use
  • Security researchers/AV quarantine copies of the package under node_modules
Response runbook
1) Triage: on each hit, read node_modules/@prompty/core/package.json (or the lockfile) to confirm the installed version is <=0.1.4 or between 2.0.0-alpha.1 and 2.0.0-beta.4 — patched builds are 2.0.0-beta.5+. 2) Contain: for confirmed-vulnerable production/internet-facing services that render community/cloned/LLM-generated .prompty files, prioritize upgrading to 2.0.0-beta.5 and restrict rendering to trusted templates. 3) Hunt: pivot each vulnerable host into UC1 (node->shell) over the same window, and review recently created/downloaded .prompty files for constructor/prototype/process/child_process traversal tokens.

| order by LastSeen desc

| order by LastSeen desc

Velocity.js SSTI RCE payload (constructor.constructor→child_process) in HTTP request AIDelivery High
Hunts inbound HTTP requests carrying the velocityjs template-injection RCE payload described in GHSA-7gfh-x38p-prh3: the property-read chain to the Function constructor plus a child_process command exec. Targets apps that render attacker-controlled Velocity templates (velocityjs ≤2.1.6) at the web/delivery layer where the payload is still visible on the wire. Rationale: Uses the exact exploit-chain strings from the PoC (constructor.constructor, process.mainModule, child_process, #set( / URL-encoded %23set() which are not present in normal Velocity rendering, giving high specificity at the delivery layer. Cross-checked against the prior fix advisory GHSA-j658-c2gf-x6pq (CVE-2026-44966) and the canonical JS-SSTI payload shape in PayloadsAllTheThings. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-j658-c2gf-x6pq • https://github.com/swisskyrepo/PayloadsAllTheThings/blob/master/Server%20Side%20Template%20Injection/JavaScript.md • https://github.com/shepherdwind/velocity.js/pull/192
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sourcesWeb.Web
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Authorized vulnerability scanners (Nessus/Qualys/Nuclei) firing SSTI templates against the app
  • Contracted penetration test or bug-bounty researcher probing template injection
  • Internal AppSec CI/CD DAST scans (Burp/ZAP) hitting the endpoint
  • A benign field/parameter legitimately containing the literal string 'child_process' (e.g. a code-snippet paste or docs form) with no Velocity markers
  • Security awareness/red-team exercises replaying known SSTI payloads
Suppression filter
| where not(csUserAgent has_any ("Nessus","Qualys","Nuclei","Burp","ZAP","Nikto"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full request (URI, body, User-Agent, source IP) and confirm the payload contains a Velocity marker (#set / %23set) alongside constructor.constructor or child_process — a raw 'child_process' with no template syntax is likely benign. Check scStatus: a 200/500 suggests the template actually rendered. 2) Contain: if source IP is not an approved scanner, block it at the WAF/edge and confirm the app's velocityjs version — upgrade to 2.1.7+ immediately if ≤2.1.6. 3) Hunt: pivot to the same host's endpoint telemetry (UC 'node process spawning shell') for the ~5 min after the request to confirm code execution; review inbound requests from the same IP for other SSTI variants and check for outbound egress from the node process.

Node.js process spawning shell / recon binary (velocityjs SSTI RCE execution) AIExploitation High
Hunts the post-exploitation result of the velocityjs RCE: a node process (node.exe / node) spawning a command shell or reconnaissance binary via child_process.execSync, as in the PoC's execSync('whoami'). A production Node web server rarely shells out to cmd/powershell/sh + whoami, making the parent→child pair high-signal. Rationale: Anchors on the PoC's execution shape — node → child_process.execSync → shell/whoami — using node(.exe) as parent, which a running web server almost never legitimately does for interactive recon binaries. Covers both the article's verified Windows platform and the more common Linux Node deployment. Corroborated by the JS-SSTI child_process technique in PayloadsAllTheThings. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-7gfh-x38p-prh3 • https://github.com/swisskyrepo/PayloadsAllTheThings/blob/master/Server%20Side%20Template%20Injection/JavaScript.md
ATT&CKT1059.003 T1059.004 T1190
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Build/tooling: node running node-gyp, webpack, or npm lifecycle scripts that shell out to cmd/sh
  • Application features that legitimately call child_process (e.g. invoking git, ffmpeg, ImageMagick, or curl) as part of normal workflow
  • Process managers (PM2, forever) or health-check scripts spawning shells
  • DevOps CI runners executing node-based build steps that call whoami/hostname for diagnostics
  • Serverless/container entrypoints where node bootstraps a shell wrapper
Suppression filter
| where not(ProcessCommandLine has_any ("node-gyp","npm","npx","webpack","prebuild","install","git ","\\git.exe"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: examine ProcessCommandLine of the child and InitiatingProcessCommandLine of node — a one-off `cmd /c whoami` / `sh -c whoami` with no build context is suspicious, whereas node-gyp/npm/git command lines are likely benign. Confirm the host runs a public-facing app using velocityjs ≤2.1.6. 2) Contain: isolate the host if the shell ran recon (whoami/ipconfig/net) with no legitimate parent chain; capture the node process command line and open sockets, then rotate any credentials/cloud tokens reachable from the app's environment. 3) Hunt: correlate to the web-layer UC for an inbound constructor.constructor/#set payload just prior; review the node process for subsequent outbound connections (reverse shell / staging), new file writes, and lateral movement from the host.

Vulnerable seroval (<=1.5.2) / TanStack Start (<1.167.30) exposure to CVE-2026-59940 deserialization RCE AIExploitation Medium
Hunts managed assets whose software inventory reports seroval prior to 1.5.3 or @tanstack/start-server-core prior to 1.167.30, the versions vulnerable to the fromJSON() Promise-resolver type-confusion that yields attacker-controlled server-side invocation. The exploit itself is deliberately non-observable in EDR (no PoC published, bypasses request middleware/audit logging and spawns no distinctive child process), so exposure hunting on the concrete package+version is the only reliable article-specific control. Rationale: Uses the exact concrete artifacts the advisory and corroborating sources fix: package `seroval` patched at 1.5.3 (vuln <=1.5.2), downstream `@tanstack/start-server-core` patched at 1.167.30, and CVE-2026-59940. No runtime IOC is emitted because the disclosure explicitly omits the PoC and the exploit bypasses middleware/audit logging with no distinctive process artifact — so exposure hunting is the only article-specific, executable detection. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-mv8w-475r-vwqw • https://cvereports.com/reports/GHSA-9M65-766C-R333 • https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-JS-SEROVAL-15054506
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.007
Data sources
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer/CI/build hosts carrying seroval as a transitive dependency but not running an internet-facing TanStack server function endpoint
  • Assets already remediated but showing stale MDVM inventory before the next scan refresh
  • Bundled/minified copies where the version string is inaccurate or absent, causing over- or under-matching on parse_version
  • Non-server workstations that have the package via an Electron/desktop app with no untrusted-client Seroval JSON ingress
  • Name-collision matches on unrelated software containing the token 'tanstack' or 'seroval' in the display name
Suppression filter
| where not (SoftwareName has "seroval" and parse_version(SoftwareVersion) >= parse_version("1.5.3")) and not (SoftwareName has "tanstack" and parse_version(SoftwareVersion) >= parse_version("1.167.30"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the reported SoftwareVersion is genuinely below the fix (seroval 1.5.3 / start-server-core 1.167.30) and check DeviceInfo.IsInternetFacing plus whether the host actually serves TanStack server functions that deserialize client JSON. 2) Contain: prioritise internet-facing hosts — upgrade to the patched versions and, as defense-in-depth, restrict accepted Seroval node types and allowlist plugin tags for inbound deserialization; isolate any endpoint that cannot be patched immediately. 3) Hunt: on confirmed-exposed hosts review app/WAF logs for POSTs to server-function routes with anomalously large or structurally unusual JSON bodies, and look for any node process spawning unexpected children as a post-deserialization side effect.

`kiota info` run against a remote/untrusted OpenAPI description (CVE-2026-59865) AIExploitation Medium
Surfaces `kiota info` invocations that read an OpenAPI descriptor from a remote/untrusted HTTP(S) source — the precondition for CVE-2026-59865, where a spec-supplied `x-ms-kiota-info.dependencyInstallCommand` replaces kiota's trusted install suggestion and can drive `curl | bash`-style RCE on the developer/CI host. Hunts the kiota process itself with `info` plus a remote `-d`/`--openapi` descriptor rather than a shell child (which the developer or VS Code extension, not kiota, executes). Rationale: Anchors on the article's concrete invocation surface — the `kiota info` subcommand plus a remote `-d`/`--openapi` descriptor — which is the exact vulnerable path (`x-ms-kiota-info.dependencyInstallCommand` is only read/surfaced by `kiota info`). Avoids the unreliable spec-URL field (no such EDR column) and instead pivots on the kiota process image + command line. Cross-checked the GHSA advisory (CVSS 9.3, fixed 1.32.5, PR #7883) and the sibling Kiota CVE-2026-41134 advisory to confirm behaviour and affected packages. Cross-checked against: • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-hq9q-27g5-qwpj • https://github.com/microsoft/kiota/pull/7883 • https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-2hx3-vp6r-mg3f
ATT&CKT1059 T1195.001 T1204
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Developer legitimately running `kiota info -d https://<trusted>/openapi.json` against a first-party/internal API spec
  • CI/CD client-generation pipelines that fetch a well-known remote descriptor (e.g. petstore.swagger.io) on every build
  • Platform teams evaluating third-party public APIs where the remote OpenAPI URL is expected
  • kiota invoked via `dotnet kiota` wrapper where the parent, not FileName, carries the 'kiota' token
  • Automated doc/SDK-refresh jobs pulling a partner's hosted OpenAPI description
Suppression filter
| where not (ProcessCommandLine has_any ("petstore.swagger.io", "raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft", "://localhost", "://127.0.0.1"))
Response runbook
1) Triage: pull the full ProcessCommandLine and resolve the `-d`/`--openapi` URL — is the host untrusted/newly-seen? Confirm the local kiota/VS Code extension version is < 1.32.5 (vulnerable). 2) Contain: on the same host/user within the next few minutes, hunt for a shell running the surfaced install command (`curl`/`wget` piped to `bash`/`sh`, `Invoke-WebRequest`|`IEX`, or `rm -rf`); if found, isolate the workstation/CI runner and reset any dev/cloud/registry credentials reachable from it. 3) Hunt: enumerate all hosts that ran `kiota info` against that URL, block the descriptor domain and any downloaded payload domain, and push the 1.32.5+ upgrade to kiota and the VS Code extension fleet-wide.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName has "kiota" or InitiatingProcessFileName has "kiota"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "info"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("-d http", "-d https", "--openapi http", "--descriptor http")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActingProcessName has "kiota" or ParentProcessName has "kiota"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has "info"
| where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any ("-d http", "-d https", "--openapi http", "--descriptor http")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, Hash
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Kiota info Invoked Against Remote OpenAPI Description (CVE-2026-59865)
id: 7b3d2e1a-9c4f-4a2b-bf1e-2d6c8a5f0e11
status: experimental
description: Detects kiota info run against a remote/untrusted OpenAPI descriptor, the precondition for CVE-2026-59865 spec-supplied dependencyInstallCommand injection.
references:
  - https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-hq9q-27g5-qwpj
  - https://github.com/microsoft/kiota/pull/7883
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/07/27
tags:
  - attack.t1059
  - attack.t1195.001
  - attack.execution
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_img:
    Image|contains: 'kiota'
  selection_cmd:
    CommandLine|contains: 'info'
  selection_remote:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - '-d http'
      - '-d https'
      - '--openapi http'
      - '--descriptor http'
  condition: selection_img and selection_cmd and selection_remote
falsepositives:
  - Developers generating a client from a trusted remote OpenAPI URL
level: medium
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @Image:(*\\kiota.exe OR *\\Kiota.exe OR *\\KIOTA.EXE) @CommandLine:*info* @CommandLine:*http*
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ImageFileName=/kiota(\.exe)?$/i CommandLine=/\binfo\b/i CommandLine=/https?:\/\//i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name="kiota.exe" OR Processes.process="*kiota*" OR Processes.parent_process="*kiota*") Processes.process="*info*" (Processes.process="*-d http*" OR Processes.process="*-d https*" OR Processes.process="*--openapi http*" OR Processes.process="*--descriptor http*") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | where NOT match(user, "\$$") | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime) | sort - lastTime

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-59865")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-59865")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

| sort - severity
